___________ test_the_ledger_covers_the_acted_population_exactly_once ___________

    def test_the_ledger_covers_the_acted_population_exactly_once():
        """The CURRENT ledger names every row it acts on exactly once, and its
        published counts are the ones SEQ 1458 item 1 required: the actionable
        naming population, the wider family audit it was derived from, and the
        controls that are checked unchanged rather than acted on."""
        import a7_key_correction as K
        ledger = K.load()
        keys = [(r["source_id"], r["gold_idx"]) for r in ledger["rows"]]
        assert len(keys) == len(set(keys)) == ledger["acted_rows"]
        assert ledger["actionable_population"] == 52
        assert ledger["family_audit_population"] == 68
        assert ledger["unchanged_controls"] == 16
        assert (ledger["actionable_population"] + ledger["unchanged_controls"]
                == ledger["family_audit_population"])
        # This is historical ledger accounting, not permission to replay its
        # corrections against the new signed key.

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):

            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with

            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",

            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:

            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks

            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()

        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])
E               ValueError: the approved key's signing gate is not clean: ["the hard-review reading hr-000/b1 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value", "the hard-review reading hr-000/b2 is unproved: the receipt is not the owner's: receipt.derived_from is not the expected value"]

            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])

    def gold_by_event():
        """The accepted gold, through the owners the A4 lock itself names.
    
        The lock records loader `build_kfields_final.v6_shards`; its key
        materializer accounts all 196 rows and stamps each accepted fact with the
        reviewer's own gold fields. Attaching those by hand would be a second,
        unreviewed key builder.
        """
        import build_kfields_final as F
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        if A5.APPROVED_KEY_DIR:
            # THE APPROVED KEY'S OWN SETTLEMENT, through the SAME owners that
            # produced its signed sidecar. The historical route below asks
            # A6.bound() for the run it was sealed against and reads it with
            # v6_shards; on this key that route does not merely disagree, it
            # cannot derive at all - it refuses with "A3 evidence is not proved",
            # because the settlement it looks for is another run's. F's own
            # signing gate and materializer are what signed this key, so they are
            # what re-derive it (Codex SEQ 1933 item 3 / 1935).
            bound = _approved_bound()
            gate = F.signing_gate(bound.events, bound)
            if not gate.get("ok"):
>               raise ValueError("the approved key's signing gate is not clean: "
                                 "%s" % (gate.get("stops") or [])[:2])

    def test_real_invalid_primary_and_valid_retry_keep_both_raw_attempts(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
        import build_a5_exp5_kit as A5
        import raw_transport as RT
        import audit_worker_access as AUD
        import g1_fake_state as FAKE
        import test_harness_guards as TG
        from pathlib import Path
    
        projects = os.environ["A7_FIXTURE_PROJECTS"]
        monkeypatch.setattr(AUD, "PROJECTS_ROOT", projects)
        native = FAKE.declared_input_record()
        assert native
        original = TG._a1_transcript
    
        def transcript(prompt, answer, model, uuid0="u0", uuid1="u1", agent_id="a01", session_id="sess"):
            records = original(prompt, answer, model, uuid0, uuid1, agent_id, session_id)
            records.insert(native["record_index"], {
                "type": native["record_type"], "uuid": "input-" + uuid0,
                "agentId": agent_id, "sessionId": session_id,
                native["payload_field"]: copy.deepcopy(native["payload"])})
            for before, after in zip(records, records[1:]):
                after["parentUuid"] = before["uuid"]
            return records
    
        monkeypatch.setattr(TG, "_a1_transcript", transcript)
        producer = str(tmp_path / "producer")
        prep = A5.prepare(producer)
        assert prep["ok"], prep.get("problems")
        plan = RT.a1_plan_for_run(producer)
        replies = {p["prompt_sha256"]: json.dumps({
            "source_id": p["source_id"], "facts": [],
            "abstentions": [{"reason": "TEST abstention"}], "continuity_hints": []})
            for p in plan["packets"]}
        bad = dict(replies)
        bad[plan["packets"][0]["prompt_sha256"]] = "{bad json"
        first = tmp_path / "primary_transport"
        first.mkdir()
        TG._a1_execute(Path(_HERE), first, prep, plan, projects, bad, "trace_primary")
        final = RT.a1_finalize(producer)
        expected = {(plan["packets"][0]["packet_id"], lane["lane_id"])
                    for lane in plan["packets"][0]["lanes"]}
        assert {tuple(k) for k in final["retry"]} == expected
        retry = RT.a1_prepare_retry(producer)
        assert retry["ok"], retry.get("problems")
        second = tmp_path / "retry_transport"
        second.mkdir()
        TG._a1_execute(Path(_HERE), second, retry, plan, projects, replies, "trace_retry")
        final_retry = RT.a1_finalize(os.path.join(producer, RT.RETRY_DIRNAME))
        assert not final_retry["retry"]
